========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I guess I have. Like, I used to sketch all the time, and I’d go to gallery openings even if I didn’t know anyone there, just to look around and feel like… connected to something. Now I don’t really care. 

Even cooking, which used to be kind of calming for me, feels pointless. I bought these nice vegetables last week and they just sat there, and then I felt bad because food waste is one of those things my mom used to lecture about—sorry, that’s not really the point. Yoga too, and tennis with my friend… I just don’t feel like doing any of it. It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t feel like mine right now.

Not really… I mean, I keep thinking I should enjoy things because they’re “my” things, you know? Like sketching used to make me feel like myself, and I’d go to gallery openings and get all excited about the weird little artist statements—sometimes they’re so dramatic it’s almost funny.

It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t feel like mine right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or foggy. Sad sounds like there’s a clear reason, like when my aunt cried for weeks after her dog died—he was this tiny terrier that hated everyone except her, actually bit the mailman once—but for me it’s not always like crying sad. It’s just kind of there. I’m not sure if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I guess sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s “often” in the way you mean. Like I’ll feel bad about little things—letting vegetables go bad, not calling someone back, that kind of thing. But then I also feel guilty for feeling guilty because it’s not like anything terrible happened, if that makes sense.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of wandering. I don’t know. I notice it here and there, but I’m not sure I’d say it’s a big constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little, I think. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I don’t weigh myself much because then I get weird about the number—well, not weird, just… I’ll keep checking it.\n\nBut my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. Like the other day I bought this muffin and only ate the top part, which I know everyone says is the best part anyway, but still. So… yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about three months? It’s hard to pin down because it didn’t start like a light switch. It was more like I kept thinking, “Oh, I’m just tired this week,” and then another week went by.\n\nBut the quieter, heavy kind of stretch—where I’m just not that interested in things and everything feels like it takes effort—yeah, that’s been going on around three months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more where you felt this flat or foggy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. I kept trying to tell myself it was just a bad couple days, or maybe the weather, or—I don’t know, my apartment has been a mess and that always makes everything feel worse.\n\nBut the flat, foggy feeling has been around for about three months, I think. Not every single second, but most of the time, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep thinking I should enjoy things because they’re “my” things, you know? I just don’t get much out of them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the percentage… I don’t really weigh myself much. I don’t know if I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really… heavy, like something you’d see on a dramatic poster with rain in the background.

I mean, I’ll sleep and still feel like unloading the dishwasher is this huge project, which sounds ridiculous because it’s just plates, but there’s always that one bowl that didn’t get clean and then I start thinking I should reorganize the cabinet—sorry. Even reading for my certificate class feels like too much sometimes, and I used to be able to push through that better.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like, I used to sketch all the time, and I’d go to gallery openings even if I didn’t know anyone there, just to look around and feel like… connected to something. Now I don’t really care. \n\nEven cooking, which used to be kind of calming for me, feels pointless. I bought these nice vegetables last week and they just sat there, and then I felt bad because food waste is one of those things my mom used to lecture about—sorry, that’s not really the point. Yoga too, and tennis with my friend… I just don’t feel like doing any of it. It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t feel like mine right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or foggy. Sad sounds like there’s a clear reason, like when my aunt cried for weeks after her dog died—he was this tiny terrier that hated everyone except her, actually bit the mailman once—but for me it’s not always like crying sad. It’s just kind of there. I’m not sure if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I guess sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s “often” in the way you mean. Like I’ll feel bad about little things—letting vegetables go bad, not calling someone back, that kind of thing. But then I also feel guilty for feeling guilty because it’s not like anything terrible happened, if that makes sense.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of wandering. I don’t know. I notice it here and there, but I’m not sure I’d say it’s a big constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little, I think. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I don’t weigh myself much because then I get weird about the number—well, not weird, just… I’ll keep checking it.\n\nBut my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. Like the other day I bought this muffin and only ate the top part, which I know everyone says is the best part anyway, but still. So… yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about three months? I kept trying to tell myself it was just a bad couple days, or maybe the weather, or—I don’t know, my apartment has been a mess and that always makes everything feel worse.\n\nBut the flat, foggy feeling has been around for about three months, I think. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Mostly I’m here because I’m just… tired. Like, not normal tired where you sleep and then you feel better. I mean, I’ll sleep and still feel like unloading the dishwasher is this huge project, which sounds ridiculous because it’s just plates, but there’s always that one bowl that didn’t get clean and then I start thinking I should reorganize the cabinet—sorry. Even reading for my certificate class feels like too much sometimes, and I used to be able to push through that better.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like, I used to sketch all the time, and I’d go to gallery openings even if I didn’t know anyone there, just to look around and feel like… connected to something. Now I don’t really care. \n\nEven cooking, which used to be kind of calming for me, feels pointless. I bought these nice vegetables last week and they just sat there, and then I felt bad because food waste is one of those things my mom used to lecture about—sorry, that’s not really the point. Yoga too, and tennis with my friend… I just don’t feel like doing any of it. It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t feel like mine right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or foggy. Sad sounds like there’s a clear reason, like when my aunt cried for weeks after her dog died—he was this tiny terrier that hated everyone except her, actually bit the mailman once—but for me it’s not always like crying sad. It’s just kind of there. I’m not sure if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I guess sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s “often” in the way you mean. Like I’ll feel bad about little things—letting vegetables go bad, not calling someone back, that kind of thing. But then I also feel guilty for feeling guilty because it’s not like anything terrible happened, if that makes sense.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of wandering. I don’t know. I notice it here and there, but I’m not sure I’d say it’s a big constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little, I think. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I don’t weigh myself much because then I get weird about the number—well, not weird, just… I’ll keep checking it.\n\nBut my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. Like the other day I bought this muffin and only ate the top part, which I know everyone says is the best part anyway, but still. So… yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about three months? It’s hard to pin down because it didn’t start like a light switch. It was more like I kept thinking, “Oh, I’m just tired this week,” and then another week went by.\n\nBut the quieter, heavy kind of stretch—where I’m just not that interested in things and everything feels like it takes effort—yeah, that’s been going on around three months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more where you felt this flat or foggy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. I kept trying to tell myself it was just a bad couple days, or maybe the weather, or—I don’t know, my apartment has been a mess and that always makes everything feel worse.\n\nBut the flat, foggy feeling has been around for about three months, I think. Not every single second, but most of the time, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep thinking I should enjoy things because they’re “my” things, you know? Like sketching used to make me feel like myself, and I’d go to gallery openings and get all excited about the weird little artist statements—sometimes they’re so dramatic it’s almost funny.\n\nBut lately I just… don’t care. Cooking feels like too many steps, yoga sounds exhausting, and even tennis with my friend, which used to be fun, I’ll just avoid or cancel. It’s not that I hate those things now. I just don’t get much out of them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the percentage… I don’t really weigh myself much. But my appetite’s been weird. Like, I’ll get to the evening and realize I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow that felt like “enough” at the time.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser, which I noticed because I have this one pair that used to be kind of unforgiving after laundry—like, you know when denim feels personally offended you have a body? Now they’re not as tight. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in how your clothes fit over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Mostly my jeans… they’ve gotten looser, and I wasn’t trying to make that happen. It’s weird because I didn’t really notice at first—I just thought maybe they’d stretched out, or maybe I’d worn them too many times before washing them, because jeans are basically a whole unpredictable science.\n\nBut then I realized it kind of fits with how I’ve been eating. Like some days I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and I’ve only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow I didn’t really feel like eating more. So… yeah, my clothes fit looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Sketching is the big one, I think. That used to be the thing that made me feel like me, like if I had a pencil and some time I could kind of settle into myself. Now I’ll look at my sketchbook and just… not care.\n\nAnd gallery openings, too. I used to like going, even if half the time I’d get distracted by people’s outfits or the tiny crackers they put out—why are they always so dry? But I used to enjoy it. Cooking, yoga… even tennis with my friend, I’ve been avoiding it. It all just feels sort of flat now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really… heavy, like something you’d see on a dramatic poster with rain in the background.\n\nI mean, I get down on myself sometimes, sure, like anyone does. Especially when I’m not doing the things I usually do, because then I start thinking, okay, what am I even doing with my time? But “worthless” feels like… I don’t know. I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll see something online—like one of those memorial posts, or a news story—and then I start thinking about how strange it is that people are just here and then not, and then somehow I end up thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer because that smell is impossible to get out. Like some days I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and I’ve only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow I didn’t really feel like eating more. So… yeah, my clothes fit looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Sketching is the big one, I think. That used to be the thing that made me feel like me, like if I had a pencil and some time I could kind of settle into myself. Now I’ll look at my sketchbook and just… not care.\n\nAnd gallery openings, too. I used to like going, even if half the time I’d get distracted by people’s outfits or the tiny crackers they put out—why are they always so dry? But I used to enjoy it. Cooking, yoga… even tennis with my friend, I’ve been avoiding it. It all just feels sort of flat now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really… heavy, like something you’d see on a dramatic poster with rain in the background.\n\nI mean, I get down on myself sometimes, sure, like anyone does. Especially when I’m not doing the things I usually do, because then I start thinking, okay, what am I even doing with my time? But “worthless” feels like… I don’t know. I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll see something online—like one of those memorial posts, or a news story—and then I start thinking about how strange it is that people are just here and then not, and then somehow I end up thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer because that smell is impossible to get out. But actual thoughts about death or dying lately? I don’t think I’ve really been having those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts that you might be better off dead?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of dealing with myself, if that makes sense—like my own brain feels like a messy drawer you keep meaning to organize, and then suddenly you’re thinking about buying drawer dividers online and it’s midnight. So… yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about three months? It’s hard to pin down because it didn’t start like a light switch. It was more like I kept thinking, “Oh, I’m just tired this week,” and then another week went by.\n\nBut the quieter, heavy kind of stretch—where I’m just not that interested in things and everything feels like it takes effort—yeah, that’s been going on around three months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more where you felt this flat or foggy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. I kept trying to tell myself it was just a bad couple days, or maybe the weather, or—I don’t know, my apartment has been a mess and that always makes everything feel worse.\n\nBut the flat, foggy feeling has been around for about three months, I think. Not every single second, but most of the time, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep thinking I should enjoy things because they’re “my” things, you know? Like sketching used to make me feel like myself, and I’d go to gallery openings and get all excited about the weird little artist statements—sometimes they’re so dramatic it’s almost funny.\n\nBut lately I just… don’t care. Cooking feels like too many steps, yoga sounds exhausting, and even tennis with my friend, which used to be fun, I’ll just avoid or cancel. It’s not that I hate those things now. I just don’t get much out of them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the percentage… I don’t really weigh myself much. But my appetite’s been weird. Like, I’ll get to the evening and realize I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow that felt like “enough” at the time.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser, which I noticed because I have this one pair that used to be kind of unforgiving after laundry—like, you know when denim feels personally offended you have a body? Now they’re not as tight. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in how your clothes fit over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Mostly my jeans… they’ve gotten looser, and I wasn’t trying to make that happen. It’s weird because I didn’t really notice at first—I just thought maybe they’d stretched out, or maybe I’d worn them too many times before washing them, because jeans are basically a whole unpredictable science.\n\nBut then I realized it kind of fits with how I’ve been eating. Like some days I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and I’ve only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow I didn’t really feel like eating more. So… yeah, my clothes fit looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Sketching is the big one, I think. That used to be the thing that made me feel like me, like if I had a pencil and some time I could kind of settle into myself. Now I’ll look at my sketchbook and just… not care.\n\nAnd gallery openings, too. I used to like going, even if half the time I’d get distracted by people’s outfits or the tiny crackers they put out—why are they always so dry? But I used to enjoy it. Cooking, yoga… even tennis with my friend, I’ve been avoiding it. It all just feels sort of flat now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really… heavy, like something you’d see on a dramatic poster with rain in the background.\n\nI mean, I get down on myself sometimes, sure, like anyone does. Especially when I’m not doing the things I usually do, because then I start thinking, okay, what am I even doing with my time? But “worthless” feels like… I don’t know. I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll see something online—like one of those memorial posts, or a news story—and then I start thinking about how strange it is that people are just here and then not, and then somehow I end up thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer because that smell is impossible to get out. But actual thoughts about death or dying lately? I don’t think I’ve really been having those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts that you might be better off dead?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of dealing with myself, if that makes sense—like my own brain feels like a messy drawer you keep meaning to organize, and then suddenly you’re thinking about buying drawer dividers online and it’s midnight. But “better off dead”… no, I don’t think I’ve been thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying more frequently than you used to?\n\nPatient: More frequently? I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve been tracking it like that.\n\nLike, if I see something about it, I might think about it for a minute, but then my mind kind of wanders off—like yesterday I was reading an article and somehow ended up looking up whether succulents can actually survive in a bathroom with no window. So… I haven’t noticed it being more often, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Keep coming back? I don’t think so… not in a way where it’s like stuck in my head.\n\nSometimes a thought will pop up if something reminds me, but then it kind of gets replaced by something completely random, like whether I paid the electric bill or why my neighbor’s dog always barks at the same time every afternoon. So no, I haven’t noticed death or dying thoughts coming back over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know if I’d say often. I mean, I can feel bad about little things, like if I forget to text someone back, and then I’ll think about how texting has gotten so complicated because now people can see when you read things—well, depending on the app—and that’s kind of stressful.\n\nBut guilty about things I wouldn’t have before? I haven’t really noticed that, no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I’ve definitely fallen behind.

My certificate coursework is piling up, and then regular things—bills, making meals, laundry, dishes—somehow all stack up because I make plans and then I don’t follow through, or I can’t keep the pieces organized. My spouse has had to pick up more at home, which makes me feel bad.

And socially… I’ve kind of pulled back. Friends, and even art stuff I usually care about. I keep meaning to reconnect, and then I get distracted or overwhelmed, like I’ll start organizing one drawer and suddenly it’s two hours later and I haven’t messaged anyone or done the thing I was supposed to do. So, yeah, it’s been affecting things.

I keep meaning to reconnect, and then I get distracted or overwhelmed, like I’ll start organizing one drawer and suddenly it’s two hours later and I haven’t messaged anyone or done the thing I was supposed to do. So, yeah, it’s been affecting things.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced a significant decrease in interest or pleasure in most activities for more than two weeks?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. More than two weeks, yeah.

I just kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like me—sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking. Even yoga, which is funny because I bought this really nice mat ages ago and was so proud of it, and then it just sits there rolled up like it’s judging me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Mostly I’m here because I’m just… tired. Like, not normal tired where you sleep and then you feel better. I mean, I’ll sleep and still feel like unloading the dishwasher is this huge project, which sounds ridiculous because it’s just plates, but there’s always that one bowl that didn’t get clean and then I start thinking I should reorganize the cabinet—sorry. Even reading for my certificate class feels like too much sometimes, and I used to be able to push through that better.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like, I used to sketch all the time, and I’d go to gallery openings even if I didn’t know anyone there, just to look around and feel like… connected to something. Now I don’t really care. \n\nEven cooking, which used to be kind of calming for me, feels pointless. I bought these nice vegetables last week and they just sat there, and then I felt bad because food waste is one of those things my mom used to lecture about—sorry, that’s not really the point. Yoga too, and tennis with my friend… I just don’t feel like doing any of it. It’s like those things are still there, but they don’t feel like mine right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. It’s more like… flat, maybe? Or foggy. Sad sounds like there’s a clear reason, like when my aunt cried for weeks after her dog died—he was this tiny terrier that hated everyone except her, actually bit the mailman once—but for me it’s not always like crying sad. It’s just kind of there. I’m not sure if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I mean, I guess sometimes, but I’m not sure if it’s “often” in the way you mean. Like I’ll feel bad about little things—letting vegetables go bad, not calling someone back, that kind of thing. But then I also feel guilty for feeling guilty because it’s not like anything terrible happened, if that makes sense.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of wandering. I don’t know. I notice it here and there, but I’m not sure I’d say it’s a big constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little, I think. I haven’t been trying to or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I don’t weigh myself much because then I get weird about the number—well, not weird, just… I’ll keep checking it.\n\nBut my appetite’s been off. Some days I’ll get to late afternoon and realize I’ve basically just had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. Like the other day I bought this muffin and only ate the top part, which I know everyone says is the best part anyway, but still. So… yeah, I think I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe about three months? It’s hard to pin down because it didn’t start like a light switch. It was more like I kept thinking, “Oh, I’m just tired this week,” and then another week went by.\n\nBut the quieter, heavy kind of stretch—where I’m just not that interested in things and everything feels like it takes effort—yeah, that’s been going on around three months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more where you felt this flat or foggy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than two weeks. I kept trying to tell myself it was just a bad couple days, or maybe the weather, or—I don’t know, my apartment has been a mess and that always makes everything feel worse.\n\nBut the flat, foggy feeling has been around for about three months, I think. Not every single second, but most of the time, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep thinking I should enjoy things because they’re “my” things, you know? Like sketching used to make me feel like myself, and I’d go to gallery openings and get all excited about the weird little artist statements—sometimes they’re so dramatic it’s almost funny.\n\nBut lately I just… don’t care. Cooking feels like too many steps, yoga sounds exhausting, and even tennis with my friend, which used to be fun, I’ll just avoid or cancel. It’s not that I hate those things now. I just don’t get much out of them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure about the percentage… I don’t really weigh myself much. But my appetite’s been weird. Like, I’ll get to the evening and realize I only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow that felt like “enough” at the time.\n\nAnd my jeans are definitely looser, which I noticed because I have this one pair that used to be kind of unforgiving after laundry—like, you know when denim feels personally offended you have a body? Now they’re not as tight. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant change in how your clothes fit over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Mostly my jeans… they’ve gotten looser, and I wasn’t trying to make that happen. It’s weird because I didn’t really notice at first—I just thought maybe they’d stretched out, or maybe I’d worn them too many times before washing them, because jeans are basically a whole unpredictable science.\n\nBut then I realized it kind of fits with how I’ve been eating. Like some days I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and I’ve only had coffee and a few bites of something, and somehow I didn’t really feel like eating more. So… yeah, my clothes fit looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. Sketching is the big one, I think. That used to be the thing that made me feel like me, like if I had a pencil and some time I could kind of settle into myself. Now I’ll look at my sketchbook and just… not care.\n\nAnd gallery openings, too. I used to like going, even if half the time I’d get distracted by people’s outfits or the tiny crackers they put out—why are they always so dry? But I used to enjoy it. Cooking, yoga… even tennis with my friend, I’ve been avoiding it. It all just feels sort of flat now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really… heavy, like something you’d see on a dramatic poster with rain in the background.\n\nI mean, I get down on myself sometimes, sure, like anyone does. Especially when I’m not doing the things I usually do, because then I start thinking, okay, what am I even doing with my time? But “worthless” feels like… I don’t know. I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll see something online—like one of those memorial posts, or a news story—and then I start thinking about how strange it is that people are just here and then not, and then somehow I end up thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer because that smell is impossible to get out. But actual thoughts about death or dying lately? I don’t think I’ve really been having those.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts that you might be better off dead?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of dealing with myself, if that makes sense—like my own brain feels like a messy drawer you keep meaning to organize, and then suddenly you’re thinking about buying drawer dividers online and it’s midnight. But “better off dead”… no, I don’t think I’ve been thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying more frequently than you used to?\n\nPatient: More frequently? I’m not sure… I don’t think I’ve been tracking it like that.\n\nLike, if I see something about it, I might think about it for a minute, but then my mind kind of wanders off—like yesterday I was reading an article and somehow ended up looking up whether succulents can actually survive in a bathroom with no window. So… I haven’t noticed it being more often, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts about death or dying that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Keep coming back? I don’t think so… not in a way where it’s like stuck in my head.\n\nSometimes a thought will pop up if something reminds me, but then it kind of gets replaced by something completely random, like whether I paid the electric bill or why my neighbor’s dog always barks at the same time every afternoon. So no, I haven’t noticed death or dying thoughts coming back over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I haven’t really noticed that, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant distress or impairment in your social or occupational functioning due to these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t think so… not in a way where it’s like stuck in my head.\n\nSometimes a thought will pop up if something reminds me, but then it kind of gets replaced by something completely random, like whether I paid the electric bill or why my neighbor’s dog always barks at the same time every afternoon. So no, I haven’t noticed death or dying thoughts coming back over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know if I’d say often. I mean, I can feel bad about little things, like if I forget to text someone back, and then I’ll think about how texting has gotten so complicated because now people can see when you read things—well, depending on the app—and that’s kind of stressful.\n\nBut guilty about things I wouldn’t have before? I haven’t really noticed that, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing significant distress or impairment in your social or occupational functioning due to these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I’ve definitely fallen behind.\n\nMy certificate coursework is piling up, and then regular things—bills, making meals, laundry, dishes—somehow all stack up because I make plans and then I don’t follow through, or I can’t keep the pieces organized. My spouse has had to pick up more at home, which makes me feel bad.\n\nAnd socially… I’ve kind of pulled back. Friends, and even art stuff I usually care about. I keep meaning to reconnect, and then I get distracted or overwhelmed, like I’ll start organizing one drawer and suddenly it’s two hours later and I haven’t messaged anyone or done the thing I was supposed to do. So, yeah, it’s been affecting things.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant decrease in interest or pleasure in most activities for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. More than two weeks, yeah.\n\nI just kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like me—sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking. Even yoga, which is funny because I bought this really nice mat ages ago and was so proud of it, and then it just sits there rolled up like it’s judging me.\n\nAnd tennis with my friend too… I usually like that, but lately even that feels like too much effort, or just not interesting the way it normally is. So, yes, I guess the interest and enjoyment has really dropped off."
